## The browser walk (2026-08-29) — three defects no test could see

Walked end to end against the real Anthropic API: sign up → create profile → paste three own
posts → build the voice brain → read ten rules beside their quotes → confirm each → activate.
Final state verified in the database: `active`, `activated_at` set, `confirmed_at` set, sha 64
chars, **10 confirmed = 10 evidence entries = 10 claim positions**. Screenshot:
`brain-confirmed-active.png` (repo root, beside the slice-1/2a walk screenshots).

**Every one of the three defects below was invisible to 1,234 green tests**, and each was
invisible for a different structural reason. That is the argument for the walk, stated concretely
rather than as a principle.

### 1. Every voice inference failed, after the vendor was paid (the serious one)

`llm.maxOutputTokens` was **1024** — sized in slice 2a when the only operation was a one-sentence
connectivity ping. A voice document with five quoted claims needs ~1,200 output tokens, so the
reply was cut off mid-object and `parseVoiceReply` refused it. The real failed run recorded
`tokens_out: 1024`, hitting the ceiling exactly; the successful one used **1214**.

The cost to the creator was not zero: `runInference` commits `model_usage` and takes its debit
BEFORE the parse (the deliberate A-7 settlement-tail order), so the attempt **consumed the included
build** and the next press would cost 50 credits — and fail identically. Diagnosed by reproducing
the call at 1024 and at 4000 and comparing.

Why no test saw it: the parse tests feed strings, and **no test had ever run the real prompt
against the real ceiling**. Both halves were individually correct.

- Fixed the sizing: `maxOutputTokens` 1024 → 4000 in the schema default and the seed, and appended
  as config **v6** on the live database (v5 byte-identical — the append-only rule held).
  It is a CEILING, not a target: a short operation still emits and is billed for a short reply.
- Fixed the CLASS: `LlmTruncatedError`, thrown from the adapter on `stop_reason === "max_tokens"`.
  The ceiling will be outgrown again as creators save more posts, and when it is, the product now
  says "the answer was cut off" rather than blaming the model's JSON.
- It reports outcome `schema_invalid` rather than a new enum value — a new `model_usage.outcome`
  is a migration on a live enum mirrored in two packages, and for the ROLLUP the two are the same
  fact. The cost (the margin dashboard cannot separate them) is recorded on the class.
- The remedy is routed by a new readable `LlmError.operatorRemedy`, following `billable`'s
  precedent exactly, because the facade exports only the base class to `app/**`. Without it,
  truncation inherited `llm_attempt_recorded`'s "try again" — advice that fails deterministically.

### 2. `/brain` was a 500 on first load — the whole surface of this slice

A `"use server"` module may export **only async functions**, and `readConfirmations` is a
synchronous exported helper. TypeScript is happy, the unit tests import it directly and pass, and
the rule is enforced by Next's compiler — so the only symptom is that the route does not compile.

**This repo already knew the rule and wrote it down**: `app/(product)/onboarding/run-state.ts`
carries the note verbatim, from slice 2a. Moved to `confirmations.ts` + `confirm-types.ts`, the
same directive-free shape that file established.

### 3. Two refusal codes were missing, so a paid failure read as "Something went wrong"

`inference_unusable` and `not_enough_posts` exist in `billing-errors.ts` and were absent from
`/onboarding`'s own `ONBOARDING_ERROR_CODES`, so they degraded to `unknown`. A creator whose posts
had just been sent to a vendor was told the product had malfunctioned.

**Third occurrence of one defect**, and the class-level guard that was built to stop it did not
fire — because its population was `inference.ts` alone, which was the whole spend path when it was
written. Slice 3 added a second (`infer-voice.ts` composing `assemble.ts`) that the scan could not
see. The population is now every source file the screen's spend path throws from, and adding a file
to that list is what a new spend path costs.

**The lesson, which is not "add the code":** a derived guard is only as wide as its population, and
a population written as a single path silently narrows the day a second path appears. The guard
looked like it covered the class and covered one instance of it.

### least-confident (declared before the gates, 2026-08-29)

**The whole-submit confirmation replaces `confirmed_fields` wholesale, so a stale second tab can
silently wipe decisions the creator already made.** `confirmVoiceFields` sets the column to exactly
what THAT form carried. Two tabs open on `/brain`: tab A ticks all ten and submits; tab B, rendered
before that and still showing zero ticks, submits and sets the array back to `[]`. It fails CLOSED
for activation (which then refuses, naming the unconfirmed positions), so nothing false is ever
activated — but the creator's work is gone with no message, and the ledger's own note above argues
*for* whole-submit on the grounds that per-field POSTs would race. Both shapes race; I picked the
one whose race loses work rather than the one whose race records a decision nobody made, and I did
not add the guard (a version/round-trip token) that would close it. This is the thing in the diff I
would bet fails first.

### An OPERATOR obligation the code cannot discharge (2026-08-29)

Raising `llm.maxOutputTokens` in the schema default and the seed fixes **fresh installs only**.
`config:migrate` merges only the keys a stored document is MISSING — every key it already has is
carried through byte-identically from `raw`, deliberately, so that a schema default can never
overwrite an operator's customised value (`migrate-config.ts:103`). `llm.maxOutputTokens` is
already present at **1024** in every database seeded before today, so the migration is a no-op
there and **every voice inference on an existing install keeps failing**, consuming each creator's
included build as it goes.

That is the right shape — this is a cost-bearing dial an operator owns, and silently rewriting it
would be the defect `mergeMissing` exists to prevent — but it means the slice is **not deployable
by shipping the code alone**. The required step, which belongs in the release checklist:

> Append a config version raising `llm.maxOutputTokens` to at least 4000 (via `/admin/config`),
> BEFORE or WITH the deploy. A voice inference on 1024 is truncated, refused, and billed.

Recorded here rather than "fixed" in code because the alternative — special-casing this one key in
`mergeMissing` — would put a schema default back in charge of an operator's value, which is the
invariant that file's docblock is built around. Verified by reading `mergeMissing`, not assumed.

## Reviewer gates, round 1 (2026-08-29) — ALL THREE BLOCKED

Three reviewers, run in the main tree rather than isolated worktrees (a worktree checks out HEAD,
which holds none of this uncommitted work — the review would have been vacuous). Seven blocking
findings. The slice was not ready, and the walk had already shown why that was plausible.

**Least-confident bet, declared before the gates:** the whole-submit confirmation race. Two of the
three reviewers traced it independently and neither found a money or an honesty consequence
(activation fails closed and names the unconfirmed positions); tenancy found it **wider** than I
did — the same UPDATE rewrites `confirmed_by`/`confirmed_at`, so a stale tab rewrites *who decided
and when*, on the only column recording that. Declaring it was worth it: it was the one finding I
had already reasoned about, and the reviewers spent their attention on the six I had not.

### The seven BLOCKs, and what each one actually was

1. **Confirm and activate had no lock** — both read-then-write with a plain SELECT. Under READ
   COMMITTED, activation reads ten confirmed positions, a concurrent confirm commits `[]`, and the
   UPDATE (whose predicate tests only ids) proceeds: `active`, stamped with a real human's
   `confirmed_by`, **zero positions confirmed**. A silent brain activation — the thing R-8 exists
   to forbid. Both now take the same `pg_advisory_xact_lock` `writeBrainDoc` takes.
2. **`/brain` bricked permanently past 50 posts.** `readVoiceBrain` resolved cited posts out of a
   50-row page against a 2,000-post write ceiling; once a cited post aged out, the page refused
   **proposed and active alike**, with no delete path and no un-activate to clear it. Now resolved
   BY CITED ID (`onboardingInputsByIds`), bounded by the document instead of by a page.
   **And my comment called the case "unreachable on the sanctioned path"** while calling 50 "the
   write-side ceiling's neighbourhood" — off by fortyfold. That sentence is why the
   fail-the-whole-page choice looked safe.
3. **The priced corpus silently truncated to 50** while the screen said "the posts you saved above",
   and the class filter ran in JavaScript *after* the page — so once slice 4 lands references, 50
   recent ones would starve own posts out entirely. Now `ownPostsNewest`, filtering on
   `input_class` **in SQL**, with `VOICE_CORPUS_MAX_POSTS` explicit and both counts reported so the
   screen can state the bound.
4. **The config fix could not reach any existing database.** `mergeMissing` adds absent keys only —
   correctly, so a schema default can never overwrite an operator's value — so every pre-slice-3
   install kept `maxOutputTokens: 1024` and kept truncating. I had recorded this as a manual
   release step; the reviewer was right that a manual-only remedy is not a fix. Now a keyed
   `CORRECTIONS` entry that fires **only from the exact value this product wrote** (1024 → 4000)
   and leaves any operator-chosen number alone — asserted in both directions.
5. **A truncation consumed the creator's included build** for a deterministic outage of ours.
   `billable` was answering two questions at once. Split: `billable` still drives the REQ-G05
   rollup, and a new `consumesIncludedBuild` (with a `model_usage.consumed_included_build` column,
   migration 0016, defaulted `true` so no existing row changes meaning) drives the entitlement.
   R14 already stated the principle one case over.
6. **R4's filter — the single line deciding which of a creator's words leave the server — had no
   test at all**, and its comment claimed a second assertion in `assembleVoicePrompt` that does not
   exist and could not (that function receives `id` and `content` only). `infer-voice.test.ts` now
   plants a `reference` row and proves neither its text nor its id reaches the provider;
   mutation-verified (removing the class filter reddens three).
7. **The paste panel told creators their posts stay on this server** — "nothing else reads them yet
   — this screen keeps them, and that is all it does" — two panels above the control that sends
   them to a vendor. A false data-handling statement at the moment of collection, and
   `stale-disclosure.test.ts`, the instrument built for exactly this class, had gained no row for
   it. Rewritten, and the row added.

### The CHANGEs, all fixed in the same pass

R10 failed open for `{isPlaceholder: false, quote: null}` — rendering a rule with no quote, no named
absence, and a checkbox saying "Yes — this is right"; the reviewer **proved it by rendering it**,
while the type's docblock claimed the pairing was unbreakable. `claimsFor` refuses that shape now ·
neither truncation branch was executed by any test (see below) · `prompt_bundle_version` booked
every voice run against `slice2a-smoke-v1` · the truncation discarded the vendor's usage, recording
the most expensive failure as `cost_state: unknown` and biasing margin upward · an empty
confirmation was accepted and stamped an attributable decision over nothing · `brain-view` printed
"You confirmed this." unconditionally, ignoring the prop beside it · the onboarding header still
described slice 2a in three false clauses · "this one stays readable either way" promised a read
path that does not exist.

### The finding that stung, and why it is recorded rather than smoothed over

**Neither new truncation branch was executed by any test.** `complete()` was invoked by no test in
`packages/llm`, so deleting the branch left the suite green — the class-level tests asserted the
error's *properties* and never that anything CONSTRUCTS it. That is verbatim the lesson written into
`CLAUDE.md` earlier the same day, broken within the hour by the person who wrote it. Both branches
are now driven through `underlyingFetch`, and the condition is mutation-verified red→green.

The general shape, which is the part worth keeping: **the two guards I added in this slice were
both un-witnessed in the same way** — R8's `attested` (caught by my own mutation run) and the
truncation branch (caught by a reviewer). Writing a guard and writing its witness are separate acts,
and finishing the first feels like finishing both.

### Copy scans: one canon instead of two

`/onboarding` and `/brain` had grown separate FORBIDDEN lists and diverged in the worst direction —
`/brain` banned `guarantee` and `confiden`; the screen that SPENDS A CREDIT banned neither, on
exactly the words REQ-I04 names. Neither banned `train`, the one word that would falsify
"context, never weights" (R-8) outright. Now one shared canon in `tests/support/forbidden-claims.ts`,
with per-word specimens so a broken pattern cannot hide behind a sibling.

### Entry gate after the fix pass

typecheck **0** · lint **0** · `db:migrate` applied (0016) · `db:check` clean ·
**1257 of 1258 / 0 skipped** across 60 files. The one failure is the same non-slice-3 marketing
route as before. Evidence: `entry-gate-slice-3-round2.txt`.

## Reviewer gates, round 2 (2026-08-29) — and where this stops

Verdicts: tenancy **NEEDS CHANGES**, compliance **NEEDS CHANGES**, billing **BLOCK**. Both round-1
BLOCKs were confirmed closed by all three, on evidence they executed rather than read — compliance
re-rendered the R10 shape and bundled probes with the repo's own esbuild; tenancy tried and failed
to force `onboardingInputsByIds` outside the cage on both axes.

### The billing BLOCK was a defect I INTRODUCED in round 1's fix

Making a truncation non-consuming was right, and it removed the only thing bounding it. Until
`consumesIncludedBuild` existed, every non-consuming outcome was also a ZERO-COST one, so
"does not consume the included build" and "costs us nothing" were the same fact and nothing needed
to count these. `LlmTruncatedError` is the first that is billable to us and free to the creator —
so `priceOf` returns 0, the balance check is skipped entirely, and truncation is deterministic:
a full output ceiling per press, forever, at our expense. **Round 1's defect had bounded it by
accident** (press two cost 50 credits nobody on Free could pay).

Fixed with `onboarding.maxUnchargedBillableAttempts` (3) and `UnchargedAttemptCapError`, refused
before the vendor and witnessed by a test that proves the fourth press never reaches a provider.
Recorded as R-48, whose closing line is the general rule: **a fix that removes an accidental bound
owes a deliberate one.**

### The same un-witnessed-guard defect, for the third and fourth time in one slice

Round 2 found *two more* guards with no witness: the confirm/activate advisory lock (deleting both
`tx.execute` lines left 1226 tests green) and the R10 refusal (deleting it left the suite green,
and it is unreachable through the database, so only a unit test can reach it). Both now have
mutation-verified witnesses. With R8's `attested` and the truncation branch, that is **four** guards
written this slice whose witness I did not write until something forced it.

### What else round 2 caught, all fixed

`ownPostsNewest` silently clamped an EXPLICIT limit — round 1's own defect one layer down, where a
raised bound would have left the read at 50 while the copy stated the new number; it refuses now ·
`llm_truncated`'s copy said "recorded and counted" when the code had just stopped counting it ·
`CORRECTIONS` matched on VALUE, so an operator who deliberately chose 1024 would have had it
quadrupled under a docblock claiming that could not happen — it now requires product provenance
(`created_by`) too · `promptBundleVersion` optional left slice 2a's retired bundle as the default on
a NOT NULL attribution column; required now, and the dead constant deleted · `PLACEHOLDER_ABSENCE`
attributed the absence to the creator's material, the exact shape `run-copy.ts` had already ruled
out — replaced with the compliance reviewer's wording so both surfaces agree · `VOICE_CORPUS_MAX_POSTS`
moved to config (R-49): 50 × 20,000 chars ≈ 250k tokens is over the context window, so a long-form
creator would have hit a permanent `BadRequestError` reading "this is our bug, not yours".

**My own new copy tripped the shared canon** on "we will" — the guard added this slice catching the
person who added it, one hour later.

### Entry gate, final

typecheck **0** · lint **0** · `db:check` clean · **1264 of 1265 / 0 skipped** across 60 files.
The one failure remains `app/(marketing)/for/[audience]/page.tsx`, an untracked route from the
concurrent marketing stream that owes its own `PUBLIC_ENTRYPOINTS` entry. Evidence:
`entry-gate-slice-3-final.txt`.

### STOPPING HERE, deliberately

`implement.md` bounds a gate at **two rounds**, and round 2 is spent. The fixes above are therefore
**unreviewed** — that is the honest status, not a formality: round 2 proved that a fix pass can
introduce a BLOCK, and this pass is larger than round 1's. A third round needs the owner's
go-ahead, asked for at the moment it is wanted rather than assumed.

Residuals the reviewers raised and I did NOT act on, each with their recommendation:
- **The confirmation round-trip token** — tenancy verified it is shippable today (nothing can edit
  `content`/`source_evidence`, so a stale tab can only LOSE ticks, never gain a false confirmation)
  and named the exact shape it needs; it becomes load-bearing at **slice 5**, which edits fields.
  Recorded as an owned obligation on slice 5 rather than built now.
- **The R8 attestation column** — tenancy's answer was *do not add it*: a boolean that can only ever
  hold `true` records nothing and would look like evidence while being none. The honest fix is to
  correct R-47's title and the copy to say the `own_post` label IS the record. **Not done** — it is
  a wording change to a recorded decision and I would rather the owner see the reviewer's reasoning
  first.
- **`app/(marketing)/for/[audience]/page.tsx`** — needs a `PUBLIC_ENTRYPOINTS` entry with a reason
  from whoever added it. It is the only red in the entry gate.
- **`safe-log` / echo-bar span leak** — `assertNoReferenceEcho` interpolates a verbatim span of a
  third party's post into a message that reaches stdout. Not live (no app surface writes a
  `reference` input) and **due at slice 4**, which is where it becomes reachable.
- **`respinCredits.runInference` has no caller** since R-46 retired the ping, and it is the one door
  outside the `own_post` cage. Delete it or give it a caller and a test.
- **`/brain` never scans its refusal copy** the way `/onboarding` does; compliance ran the scan by
  hand and found it clean today, so this is a missing guard rather than bad copy.
- **The corpus bound is still not stated on screen** — `postsUsed`/`postsAvailable` are computed and
  tested and then dropped before the view. Compliance's answer: render it only when it binds, and
  state it unconditionally in the PRE-PRESS sentence, which currently promises "the posts you saved
  above".

## Slice 3 — the close-out pass (2026-08-29, on the owner's go-ahead)

The owner read the stopping report and said to close the slice. Read as three answers: spend the
third gate round on the unreviewed fixes; make the R-47 wording change; leave the marketing route's
`PUBLIC_ENTRYPOINTS` entry to its author (the guard's own rule — declaring somebody else's route
public on their behalf is the "inherited by omission" it exists to prevent).

- 2026-08-29 — **the corpus bound is on screen, both halves, per compliance's recommendation.**
  `preSendSentence(corpusMax)` states the ceiling UNCONDITIONALLY before the press (config-null says
  a ceiling exists and could not be shown — never the old unbounded claim), and
  `corpusBoundSentence(postsUsed, postsAvailable)` renders after a run ONLY when the bound bound.
  The two counts now travel from `InferVoiceResult` through the action's return value — the action
  is where they had been dying — and the `VoiceInferenceState` structural pin was widened by exactly
  the two numeric fields, keeping its no-model-prose property. New states are IN the R12 scans
  ("a clamped corpus" outcome; the view scan reads the real `preSendSentence` through the fixture),
  and the digits are asserted, not the prose (the `pageSize` lesson).
- 2026-08-29 — **`respinCredits.runInference` is DELETED** (the tenancy residual). R-46 retired its
  only rendered caller, leaving it a caller-less POST-reachable door to the model with an ARBITRARY
  prompt — the one door outside the `own_post` cage. The internal `runInference` is untouched (it is
  the spend spine `inferVoice` composes); `RunInferenceParams` left with the method,
  `RunInferenceResult` stays (it is `InferVoiceResult.run`); the scope-cage registry shrank by
  exactly the one entry, with the reason recorded beside the internal function's surviving entries.
- 2026-08-29 — **`/brain` scans its refusal copy** (the compliance residual): every
  `BRAIN_ERROR_CODES` entry through `brainErrorFor` — title AND detail, overrides applied — against
  the shared canon, with a per-scan non-vacuity probe. The instrument compliance ran by hand is now
  a test.
- 2026-08-29 — **R-47's title corrected** to "the `own_post` label IS the record of the
  attestation", with a dated correction paragraph adopting the tenancy reasoning verbatim: no
  `attested` column — a boolean that can only hold `true` records nothing and would LOOK like
  evidence; the label exists only because a caller asserted authorship through the no-default
  parameter, so the label is the record. The verification scope limit stands unchanged.
- 2026-08-29 — **a real test-vs-test race, found by this pass's first full run and fixed at the
  cause.** `import-boundary.test.ts`'s non-vacuity case plants `packages/credits/src/__cage_probe.ts`
  for the lifetime of one assertion; `isolation.test.ts`'s module-enumeration walk reads that
  directory from a CONCURRENT worker, so a well-timed run reported the planted probe as an
  unenumerated credits module (3 failures in one run, 1 in the next — same tree). An interrupted
  earlier run had also stranded the probe past its `finally` (the `__p6_probe.ts` shape again; the
  stray was preserved to the scratchpad before removal). The walk now excludes the documented
  `__*_probe.ts` convention — the convention, not the filename, mirroring the tsconfig exclusion —
  with the race and the reservation recorded at the site.
- 2026-08-29 — entry gate, CI shape, Docker live: typecheck **0** · lint **0** · `db:check` clean ·
  **1271 of 1272 / 0 skipped** across 60 files · `next build` **0**. The one red is
  `(marketing)/for/[audience]/page.tsx`, confirmed by running `gate-completeness` alone — the
  concurrent marketing stream's untracked route, still its author's to declare. Evidence:
  `entry-gate-slice-3-close.txt`.
- 2026-08-29 — **gate round 3 launched: 3 reviewers** (tenancy, billing, compliance), scoped to the
  two unreviewed fix passes only, read-only against the main tree with the no-mutation instruction

## Reviewer gates, round 3 (2026-08-29) — ZERO BLOCKS, and the close-out fix pass

Verdicts: tenancy **NEEDS CHANGES** (0 BLOCK · 2 CHANGE · 3 NOTE, Almost/B) · billing
**NEEDS CHANGES** (0 BLOCK · 2 CHANGE · 3 NOTE, Almost/B) · compliance **NEEDS CHANGES**
(0 BLOCK · 1 CHANGE · 2 NOTE). Every prior BLOCK and every named residual was confirmed closed —
by execution where executable: tenancy ran the confirm/activate lock witness live; billing drove
the cap's fourth press into the throwing stub and ran both Docker money suites (12/12); compliance
re-rendered 197 states and caught a planted violation with the new scans. **Round-2's BLOCK fix
(the uncharged-attempt cap) is confirmed real: counted on both cage axes, refused before the
vendor, witnessed non-vacuously.**

Per the pack's rule, CHANGE-and-below findings are **fixed without re-review** — this pass, each
verified red→green by its own test where one exists:

1. **The three 50s are ONE ceiling, enforced not narrated** (tenancy C1): the schema max, the
   accessor's `ONBOARDING_PAGE_MAX` refusal, and `VOICE_CORPUS_MAX_POSTS` were three literals that
   happened to agree, under docblocks claiming a tie. `infer-voice.test.ts` now asserts the
   constant equals the page max and that the schema refuses `ceiling + 1` — drift is a red test,
   not an operator-shaped permanent refusal.
2. **"or write it yourself" deleted from `PLACEHOLDER_ABSENCE`** (tenancy C2): the confirm screen
   hosts no edit box until slice 5; the sentence directed creators to an act the product cannot
   host. The clause returns with the affordance.
3. **`preSendSentence` corrected** (compliance C1): "each one quoting the post it came from" was
   false on the placeholder branch its own sibling copy acknowledges. Now "each one shown beside
   the quote it came from, or marked as unknown when we cannot point to one."
4. **"recent" deleted from both `uncharged_attempt_cap` refusals** (billing C1): the count is
   LIFETIME over an append-only table — no window, no per-profile clearing act. The accepted
   clearing mechanism (operator fixes the cause, raises the global cap) and the self-healing
   upgrade path (count at-or-after the active config version) are recorded as an addendum to
   **R-48**; the copy no longer implies the cap ages out.
5. **The cap's config-not-literal witness** (billing C2): the existing witness drove the SEEDED
   cap of 3, so a planted literal `3` stayed green. A new case stores cap=1 through
   `appendConfigVersion` and proves the SECOND press refuses — the concurrency-limit precedent,
   applied the day the gap was named.

NOTEs, all taken: the confirm/activate lock witness's `pg_locks` poll is now scoped to THIS
database and THIS key (both call sites — cluster-wide counting could go green off a neighbour
suite's contention, a mutation-witness flaking toward green; re-proven live, 16/16 Docker);
the probe-exclusion regex gained its own per-specimen non-vacuity and `packages/credits`'s own
tsconfig now excludes `__*_probe.ts` (the root exclusion never covered the package's own
compile); the `inferVoice` isolation waivers now name the two caged accessor reads they had
omitted; the cap check's docblock states the race bound's real width (overshoot ≤ the tier's slot
limit within one burst, self-closing on commit — accepted, with the exact-count alternative
rejected because it charges where a refusal is owed); `preSendSentence(null)` is its own scanned
state; and the two-read `postsUsed`/`postsAvailable` timing is recorded at the site (a race can
delay the bound sentence by one run, never make it overclaim).

## Slice 4 — References + the R-3 promise

- 2026-08-29 — started (dependency gate: slice 3 proven on disk — `respin-finish-slice-3-card.md`, closed at Ready). Checkpoint snapshot taken (`Checkpoints: on`): `git stash push --include-untracked` + `git stash apply --index`, tree byte-identical before/after.
- 2026-08-29 — **built.** `appendReferencePost` as a sibling of `appendOwnPost` (`onboarding-ops.ts`) — no `attested` parameter, optional `sourceUrl`, its own `REFERENCE_COUNT_MAX` (50) checked ahead of the shared `POST_COUNT_MAX`. G-11 Layer A: `referenceBudgetKey` — sha256 over `echoComparisonForm` further trimmed and whitespace-collapsed (`budgetComparisonForm`) — replaces `content_sha256` as the budget's aggregation key; `content_sha256` itself untouched. G-11 Layer B: `groupReferenceInputs` — union-find over the profile's live reference corpus, folding Layer A's exact-form matches and `findEchoWindow`'s shared-span matches into one bucket map, computed once per write and shared between `validateSourceEvidence`'s new spans and `retainedReferenceSpans`'s retained spans. G-16: `isUsableSpanRange` is now the one predicate both `assertUsableSpan` (echo.ts, refuses a bad NEW span) and `retainedReferenceSpans` (with-workspace.ts, now SKIPS a bad STORED span instead of only checking `Number.isInteger`) share. G-10: a third bijection direction added in `writeBrainDoc` — every CITED evidence entry must name a claim position holding a STATED value, never `[check]`. R-30.10: `REFERENCE_BARRED_KINDS` widened `{voice}` → `{voice, killtest, performance_meta}`; `strategy` stays exempt (REQ-D04). New `ReferenceEchoError` class (errors.ts) separates the R-3 echo/budget refusal from the generic `ProvenanceError` quote-mismatch refusal, with its own `reference_echo` code and copy in `billing-errors.ts`. UI: a second onboarding panel ("Add posts you admire") — paste + optional source URL, no attestation control, its own count sentence against `REFERENCE_COUNT_MAX` — wired through `addReferencePostAction`; the "Your posts" list now filters to `own_post` only, and a companion reference list reads separately (own-post page window and reference-post window are different sizes, so a shared read would silently truncate one of them).
- 2026-08-29 — **least-confident: the coordinate-space mixing inside `assertReferenceQuoteBudget`'s per-bucket union.** Caught by my own new tests, not by a reviewer: when Layer B buckets TWO DIFFERENT `onboarding_inputs` rows (a full reference post and a separately-pasted excerpt of it), their UTF-16 offsets are two different coordinate spaces, and the original `group()`/`unionLength` pair unioned raw numeric ranges ACROSS `inputId`s as if they measured the same axis — silently UNDER-counting whenever two different rows' offsets happened to overlap numerically. Fixed by unioning WITHIN one `inputId` (safe, same coordinate space) and SUMMING across `inputId`s within a bucket (the conservative direction — can only over-count material genuinely from the same post, never under-count). A second self-found defect: `referenceBudgetKey`/`groupReferenceInputs`'s Layer A originally keyed on `echoComparisonForm` alone, which does not trim or collapse whitespace (` ` is outside both `\p{Cf}` and `\p{Default_Ignorable_Code_Point}`) — so the register's own running example ("a trailing space mints a fresh budget") was NOT closed by the first version. Fixed with `budgetComparisonForm` (`echoComparisonForm` + `.trim()` + whitespace-run collapse), used by both `referenceBudgetKey` and `groupReferenceInputs`'s Layer A dedup.
- 2026-08-29 — entry gate PASS, CI shape, Docker live, `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set: **67 files / 1379 tests / 1378 passed / 0 skipped**. The one failure is the pre-existing `(marketing)/for/[audience]/page.tsx` gate-completeness gap recorded in slices 2b and 3's ledger entries as belonging to a concurrent marketing stream, not this slice. `db:check`: clean (no migration — `input_class` already had `reference`, `source_url` already existed, matching the phase card's prediction). `next build`: clean, `ƒ /onboarding` compiles.
- 2026-08-29 — mutation-plantable population named, not yet run as a formal round: `M1` (budget key reverts to `content_sha256`) — the "Layer A: a trailing-space duplicate shares ONE budget" test in `echo.test.ts` is its witness. `M2` (bucket disabled) — the excerpt-sharing tests in `echo.test.ts` and `groupReferenceInputs`'s own tests. `M3` (`[check]`-position entries permitted again) — the G-10 test in `activate.test.ts`. `M4` (`REFERENCE_BARRED_KINDS` reverted) — `reference-echo.test.ts`'s per-kind cases. `M5` (never-brick short-circuit removed) — the "never-brick re-proved on the bucketed path" test in `echo.test.ts`. `M6` (reference intake reuses `appendOwnPost` with a class parameter) — `appendReferencePost`'s own dedicated function plus `table-writers.test.ts`'s writer enumeration (still one writer, `appendOnboardingInput`, for both callers — a class-parameter mutation would not change that count, so this mutation's real witness is R1's design itself: there is no `input_class` parameter to pass). `M7` (`assertUsableSpan` permissive on inverted ranges) — the reference-quote-budget "refuses an INVERTED range" test (pre-existing, still exercises the shared predicate). `M8` (refusal copy → "Something went wrong") — the R12/R13 forbidden-word scan in `onboarding-ui.test.tsx`. **Not independently verified by planting and running each mutation this session** — recorded as a residual for the reviewer gate to weigh, consistent with this project's standing lesson that a matrix authored by the same person who wrote the guards is blind to the same classes of gap.

- 2026-08-31 — **`pnpm -C respin db:check` DOES NOT DETECT SCHEMA-VS-MIGRATION DRIFT, and `CLAUDE.md`'s Commands block describes it as if it does.** Stage A reported this; it is recorded here only because it was then **verified by planting one**: a `plantedDriftProbe` column was added to `generations` in `generation-schema.ts`, present in no migration, and `db:check` printed **"Everything's fine 🐶🔥"**. The plant was reverted and the file confirmed byte-identical by SHA-256 (`495bd684…5b4034`). `drizzle-kit check` validates the migration folder's INTERNAL consistency only. The command that actually answers "does the schema match the committed migrations" is **`db:generate` reporting "No schema changes, nothing to migrate"**, which Stage A ran clean. This means every prior slice's "`db:check` clean" line in this ledger is a **weaker claim than it reads as** — it never was the drift check. This is the 2026-08-21 fail-open class one level up: a check that cannot fail on the condition it is cited for is indistinguishable from one that passes.
- 2026-08-31 — **Stage A deviations, recorded rather than absorbed.** (1) Migration is **0020**, not the card's 0017. (2) `export.ts` was NOT edited — slice 5 already made the export population registry-derived, so the registry entry plus an `exportPage` branch was the whole change; **named gap:** the *markdown* projection still walks `brain_docs` only, which matches its own stated partial-projection contract but is flagged, not silently decided. - 2026-08-31 — **R14c shipped PARTIAL, and stage C stopped rather than write a migration it was not authorised for.** What ships: a retry of a **settled** attempt replays the stored record with no vendor call and no second debit; a crash after outbound HTTP marks `recovery_required` (operator-visible, creator told nothing was charged, vendor never re-called); a retry landing on a live `vendor_complete` raises `GenerationInFlightError`. - 2026-09-01 — **Widening the predicate forced two follow-on fixes, each of which was itself a latent wrong answer.** (1) **Cycle groups:** `settle` and `observeExistingClaim` are mutually recursive, so the per-entry "every caller is covered" rule deadlocked and declared BOTH uncovered — the backward rule now runs over Tarjan SCCs and is covered when at least one caller from outside the group is covered, while **an isolated cycle is still not covered**, because unreachable-today is a weaker claim than scoped. (2) **Callee resolution is same-file-first:** `packages/modes/src/pipeline.ts:190` has its own private `settle`, so pure by-name resolution invented an edge that exists in no program, making `runGeneration` a caller of `generate.ts:settle`. Both directions are pinned by probes.
- 2026-09-01 — **The probe-artifact defect is fixed at the class and MEASURED in both directions.** `walkCodeFiles` skips probe artifacts by default with an explicit opt-in for the non-vacuity tests that need their own probe found; every probe write moved inside its `try`; `.gitignore` gained the three patterns. **With both fixes reverted and all six probes planted: `git status` listed all six as committable and `import-boundary.test.ts` went 5 tests RED naming probe files as offenders — including the exact `P6b — ... casts to VerifiedWorkspaceId` failure stage B reported and could not reproduce.** With the fixes in place and the same six probes planted: **17 scanning suites, 355 tests, all green, and `git status` shows nothing.** The diagnosis is therefore confirmed by experiment rather than by inference.
- 2026-09-01 — **A fail-open the brief did NOT anticipate, found by E2 and verified against the installed git 2.52: `git grep --untracked` does not search IGNORED files.** So gitignoring the probes — the fix for one half of the defect — would have silently blinded the five `scanFor` guards to their own non-vacuity probes, leaving three "the scan finds a planted violation" tests passing by finding nothing. `scanFor` gained an `includeIgnored` argument running a second **path-scoped** `git grep --no-exclude-standard`; path-scoped deliberately, because adding the flag at the `packages` root walks each package's `node_modules` — **measured at 87 seconds and four false offenders**. This is the exact shape of the lesson the fix was written to honour, appearing inside the fix.
- 2026-09-01 — **Residual, measured rather than assumed:** a leftover `app/__scan_probe__/probe.ts` is now invisible to every scan, to git, **and to `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` (both run with it planted, both exit 0)** — `tsconfig`'s `**/__*_probe.ts` exclude does not cover it, because the file inside the directory is named `probe.ts`. Its only remaining cost is a stray file in one working tree. A "no probe exists on disk" guard is deliberately NOT written: it would race the tests that plant them. Recorded in `probe-artifacts.ts`.
- 2026-09-01 — **Stage E2's declared weakest bet:** same-file-first callee resolution is a repo-wide change to a shipped guard's call graph. It is justified by one real collision, proven in both directions by fixtures, and leaves the live pin unchanged — but if a file ever holds two entries with the same name (an inner closure named like its outer helper), a call now resolves to the sibling instead of the cross-file target, and **neither the probes nor the live tree exercise that case**.
- 2026-09-01 — **Control-byte sweep re-run over the FINAL slice-6 surface: 190 files, 0 with control bytes** — and the zero is trustworthy because the sweep **plants a `\x01`, asserts it detects it, and only then sweeps, in one run**. The first version of this sweep was fail-open (`grep -P` under `LC_ALL=C`, which errors in this environment and printed nothing), so the paired proof is not ceremony here; it is the thing that caught the check.
- 2026-09-01 — **entry-gate PASS (slice 6 close), CI shape with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set and Docker live, evidence `entry-gate-slice-6-close.txt`:** typecheck 0 across all six projects, `eslint .` 0, **101 files / 2199 tests / 2199 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped**, `next build` compiled, and **`db:generate` reports "No schema changes, nothing to migrate"** — R-63's real drift check, run alongside `db:check` rather than instead of it, with both recorded so the difference is visible. Baseline entering slice 6 was 82 files / 1763 tests; the slice adds **19 files and 436 tests**. Zero skips means both Docker concurrency suites RAN.

## Slice 6 — reviewer gates, round 1 (2026-09-01): ONE BLOCK, and three findings two reviewers reached independently

Four reviewers, run as four separate specialists on the MAIN TREE. **Two deviations from the card's "Done when", both deliberate.** (1) The card says reviewers run "in isolated worktrees"; a worktree reviews `HEAD`, and **nothing in slices 1-6 is committed to this branch**, so worktree reviewers would have reviewed code predating the entire slice — the deviation slices 3 and 2b-c both recorded and took. (2) `Gate intensity: lean` would have merged spin compliance and learning honesty into one run; they were run as separate specialists instead. That **exceeds** the lean bar rather than thinning it, and avoided pasting two checklists verbatim into a merged brief. Vindicated by the result: the two would-be-merged reviewers produced the BLOCK and the R21 disproof respectively, and **disagreed with each other** (below).

| Gate | Verdict | Findings |
|---|---|---|
| Respin billing & credits | NEEDS CHANGES | 0 BLOCK · 5 CHANGE · 3 NOTE — Almost / B |
| Respin brain tenancy | NEEDS CHANGES | 0 BLOCK · 3 CHANGE · 3 NOTE — Almost / B |
| Respin spin compliance | **BLOCK** | 1 BLOCK · 6 CHANGE · 1 NOTE — **Not yet / D** |
| Respin learning honesty | NEEDS CHANGES | 0 BLOCK · 5 CHANGE · 3 NOTE — Almost / C |

### The BLOCK: a hard rule a model can switch off with a token the prompt tells it to write

`packages/modes/src/traceability.ts:320` scopes the `[check]` exemption to the **whole sentence unit**, so one marker anywhere in a sentence exempts every specific in it. The reviewer did not argue this — it **ran the real pipeline** and put four invented specifics onto a draft returned as `status: "usable"` with `hardRules: []` and `traceability: []`: `$4,000`, `11`, `3` and `2019`, each in a sentence carrying one `[check]`. `assemble.ts`'s `REWRITE_INSTRUCTION` **specifically invites the model to add that token on the rewrite** — the path most likely to produce the shape. This is `packages/modes`' own R5 hazard ("a hard integrity rule decided by a model is a rule that can be talked out of firing") realised through the exemption rather than through routing, and it violates non-negotiable 6 directly. **2,199 green tests did not see it** because the existing witness only covered a marker attached to the *sole* specific in a sentence.

### Three findings TWO reviewers reached independently — the strongest signal in the set

1. **The stranded `vendor_complete` row falsifies R-65's "provably transient"** (tenancy + billing). The equality CHECK bounds which STATE may hold generated output text; **nothing bounds how long a row stays in that state**, the only thing that clears one is a retry with the same `attemptId`, and `studio/actions.ts:76` mints a fresh `randomUUID()` per press. So a crash between checkpoint and settlement parks a full generated draft **permanently** in a table flagged `holdsCreatorContent: false` and excluded from the REQ-A04 export. **Billing found the third consequence neither other reviewer saw: the orphan is not counted by R16's bound either**, because its draft `model_usage` rows carry `consumed_included_build: true`. Invisible to the export warrant, to operators, and to the cap.
2. **Performance/guarantee claims on MODEL-AUTHORED text are enforced only by a prompt line** (learning + compliance). `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS` is scanned over static copy only; nothing checks `whyThisPerforms.reasoning`, hook text or `disclosure.guidance`, all rendered verbatim. `GENERATION_SYSTEM` says nothing about disclosure at all, so "most people skip the label on a short like this" would render under a **Disclosure** heading as the product's own advice.
3. **`/usage`'s period is mislabelled** (learning + billing) — and billing found the worse half: `burn-period.ts:11-13` **explicitly parked** the dead-subscription anchor with the words *"Free mints no credits today (DL-2/R-21 is slice 6's job), so nothing is being measured against the wrong anchor in the meantime"*, and **slice 6 landed the mint without revisiting it**. A former subscriber now sums many months of burn under "credits spent this month".

### The two reviewers DISAGREED about R21, and the one that EXECUTED was right

Spin compliance judged R21's replacement assertions "**strong, not decorative** — I could not weaken it **by inspection**". Learning honesty rebuilt `markerRegion` + `must` verbatim from the harness and **ran it against a hand-built lying screen: SEVEN OF EIGHT assertions passed** a screen with empty hooks, a label-only weakest point, an empty verdict and a balance-free charge line (the eighth failed only on capitalisation). Two instances proven by measurement: assertion 8's region contains only the `<h3>What the checks found</h3>` heading, and assertion 5's `/weakest point/i` is matched by the hard-coded `<strong>` label — **inside the comment block that claims `/\S/` was rejected for exactly that reason**. The underlying properties are still witnessed by other tests, so no guard is open; but R21's replacement assertions are weaker than the ban removal was supposed to buy. **Executed evidence beats inspection, and this is the record of it.** The orchestrator's own earlier report that R21 "was actually discharged" was taken from the building stage's self-report and was wrong in the same way.

### What round 1 confirmed rather than assumed

Billing traced the money spine and found it sound: one debit, one transaction, one constraint; zero balance refused **before** the vendor with the top-up prompt; `model_usage` written on success **and every failure class**; atomicity proven on real Postgres by draining the balance from a second connection mid-flight. It also **retired declared weakest bet #2**: the exactly-once property does not rest on the unproven half — with the in-lock re-read deleted, a second settlement is refused three times over (the `state='vendor_complete'` WHERE, `generations_attempt_uq`, and `credit_ledger_inference_debit_uq`). The lock buys a clean replay, not the exactly-once. Weakest bet #3 (`nonTerminalClaim`) was confirmed unreachable and its reporting judged the right call by two reviewers independently. Tenancy found the cage genuinely strong — every new read/write scoped, `assertScoped` first, composite FKs NOT NULL on both halves, viewer refused at three depths, no prompt text in `model_usage` — and noted `burnByMode` correctly places `state = 'settled'` in the **ON** clause rather than the WHERE, which would have silently inner-joined the `nonTerminalClaim` bucket away.

### Round-1 fix pass: three agents on disjoint surfaces

`packages/modes` (the BLOCK + 5 precision findings), `packages/credits`+`packages/db`+`/usage` (the money and lifecycle findings, and the R-65 correction), and `/studio`+`tests` (the R21 guard class and the incomplete override population). Reviewer spend, round 1: **4 agents**.
- 2026-09-01 — **Round-1 fix pass, group 3 (studio surface + R21 guards) complete, and its first act was to MEASURE the finding rather than accept it.** Rebuilding the harness verbatim, **7 of 8 assertions passed the lying screen** — only `studio-traceability-note` rejected it. All eight patterns were rewritten to demand the **interpolated half**: the number, the sentence body, the text inside the element. The lying screen is now a permanent fixture asserting on the **whole failure list** (`.sort()` equality, not `arrayContaining`) with `failures.filter(f => f.endsWith("not rendered"))` asserted empty — **every rejection is on content, not on a missing marker**, which is the difference between the guard R21 needed and the guard it had.
- 2026-09-01 — **The R21 fix went to the CAUSE, and found two live defects in the product on the way.** `markerRegion` was too narrow for containers — the same fixed-window shape its own comment described — and is now bounded by the **element** (opening tag to matching close, `null` on unbalanced markup), witnessed by three planted shapes. The `:167-174` comment that claimed a property its pattern lacked was rewritten to name the measurement. A content witness was added for the one axis that had none (`killTestSentence` rendered verbatim). And two defects nobody had reported: **`generation-outcome.tsx`'s replay branch gated on a truthy `state.weakestPoint`, so `" "` rendered the "Weakest point:" label above nothing** — the exact label-above-a-blank shape the reviewer proved the *test* was vulnerable to, live in the *product* — and `creatorRulesSentence(true, 1)` read "1 kill-test criterion **were** scored". Both fixed with witnesses.
- 2026-09-01 — **THREE of eleven planted mutations initially SURVIVED (M7, M10, M11), and were only reddened after their witnesses were written.** The author recorded this as "the 2026-08-26 rule landing on my own work". That is the correct reading and it is the second time in this slice a self-planted matrix has produced its own counter-example — the value of the matrix is what it FAILS to redden, not the score.
- 2026-09-01 — **The refusal-copy population is now derived from `STUDIO_ERROR_CODES` over THREE false-copy classes** (the onboarding entitlement, the fixed-ping privacy claim, and upgrade-as-remedy — the third added by the fixer). All **34** codes re-audited and clean; the two the hand-written list missed are asserted **overridden, not merely clean**, with the shared copy asserted to still contain the false clause so a code cannot be dropped from the set to fake a green. **One further violation was found outside the studio set and fixed at the source rather than exempted:** `not_chargeable` said *"we will not attempt an automatic charge"* — `we will` in the negated form — and was reworded, so the canon is not loosened for every screen to spare one sentence.
- 2026-09-01 — **`/usage` gained the honesty scan it never had, and the scan immediately found two real canon violations in shipped copy.** Because `/usage` was not that agent's surface, they were RECORDED in an `OPEN_FINDINGS` ratchet — each entry asserted to be **still present in the source** (so a fix goes red telling you to delete the entry), each asserted to **genuinely break the canon** (so no entry can quietly exempt clean copy), the exemption scoped to the **sentence not the word** (proved by a planted second occurrence), and the count pinned. **Both were then fixed in this session** — `"…and we will look at it."` dropped, and `"…is still accurate."` became `"…is read separately and is unaffected."` — the ratchet went red on both exactly as designed, and the list is now empty with its count pinned at **0**.
- 2026-09-01 — **Emptying that ratchet exposed a fail-open in the ratchet's own test, fixed in the same change.** `withoutOpenFindings` closed over the live list, so the "the exemption is the SENTENCE, never the word" test **stopped asserting anything the moment the list reached zero** — a scan with nothing to find being indistinguishable from a broken one, 2026-08-21, one level up. The helper is now parameterised and that test drives a **synthetic** list, so the mechanism stays proven while no real finding is open; a second test asserts the LIVE list is genuinely empty and names what it hides if it ever regrows. 25 tests green.
- 2026-09-01 — **The cross-package vocabulary agreement landed as a test, not a hook, and it is imported BY PATH.** `tests/claims-vocabulary-agreement.test.ts` reads `../packages/modes/src/claims` rather than the package name, so `@respin/modes` stays unresolvable from `app/**` and R-64 keeps **both** its lint denial and its module-resolution denial. Asserted as a **superset, not an equality** — performance family id-for-id, source-and-flags and specimen **in order**; the 8 extras **named, not counted**; every shape non-vacuous against its own specimen; no `/g` flag. Six drift mutations redden. `FLAG_ONLY_FIELD_PREFIXES`, a second hand-copied list the group-1 fix created, was bound the same way.
- 2026-09-01 — **AN INCIDENT, self-reported rather than discovered, and verified independently before being accepted.** While reverting a planted mutation the fixer ran `git checkout -- tests/support/forbidden-claims.ts`. That file is **tracked**, so it reverted to the last commit and **wiped every slice-6 addition to it** (`PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS`, `STUDIO_POSITIVE_ASSERTIONS`, the slice-6 `CLAIM_SPECIMENS`); in the same command `generation-outcome.tsx` is **untracked**, so its checkout failed and **two planted mutations stayed applied**. One command, both failure directions at once. It reconstructed both files and switched the harness to file-copy backups with a SHA revert check. **Independently verified in this session rather than taken on trust:** `forbidden-claims.ts` carries `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS`, `STUDIO_POSITIVE_ASSERTIONS`, all **8** marker entries and the `NOT_BUILT_YET` slice-6 note; a repo-wide grep for `__mutated`/`MUTATION_PLANT`/`__planted` over `app/`, `tests/` and every `packages/*/src` returns **nothing**. **The lesson is the general one: `git checkout --` is not a mutation-revert tool in a tree where the work is uncommitted** — for a tracked file it reverts far more than the mutation, and for an untracked one it silently does nothing at all.
- 2026-09-01 — **LITERAL CONTROL BYTES IN SOURCE, THE THIRD OCCURRENCE IN THIS SLICE — and this one shipped in guards that PASSED EVERYTHING.** Three of group 3's own `.not.toMatch` regexes were written with a backspace byte where `\b` was meant (a heredoc ate the backslash) and matched nothing. **It was caught by the `no-control-regex` lint rule, not by any test**, and the author reported honestly that they had **not audited every negative assertion for the same class**. So the sweep was re-run repo-wide, over 276 files, with its non-vacuity plant first: **2 files carried control bytes, both benign and both confirmed by reading them** — `safe-log.test.ts`'s NUL and ESC are *deliberate hostile fixtures* for the `wireLabel` clamp, and the third was a comment in `usage-honesty.test.tsx` **describing the lost-backslash defect, which had lost its own backslash**. That comment is fixed. Three independent agents committed this defect in one slice; twice the author caught it, once a lint did, and the residue only came out of a byte-level sweep — which is the case for the sweep being a standing check rather than a one-off.
- 2026-09-01 — **Group 3's declared weakest bet, carried into round 2:** the `studio-hooks` pattern `/<li\b[^>]*>\s*<div>\s*[^<\s]/` is **coupled to the component's DOM shape, not to its meaning** — it passes because each hook's text happens to sit in a bare `<div>`. A purely cosmetic refactor would redden it with nothing wrong, and there is no proof it could not be satisfied by some other `<div>`-opening list item a future layout produces. It rejects today's lying screen; it is the weakest of the eight as a statement about *meaning*.
- 2026-09-01 — **The post-fix entry gate went RED with 2352 of 2352 tests PASSING, and the red was right.** `pnpm -C respin test` reported `Test Files 104 passed · Tests 2352 passed · **Errors 2**` and exited non-zero with `ELIFECYCLE Test failed` — an **unhandled rejection**: `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "credit_ledger_free_allowance_uq"`, `Key (workspace_id, ref_id)=(…, 2026-08) already exists`. It was NOT the reporter-RPC exit-code flake slice 2a recorded, and it was not treated as one.
- 2026-09-01 — **Root cause, found rather than assumed: a racing test helper that owned the failure it existed to cause one `await` too late.** `packages/db/tests/generation-schema.docker.test.ts`'s `raceOneKey` bound `const second = c2.query(...)` **without awaiting it**, then ran `await c1.query("COMMIT")` before `await second`. That COMMIT is precisely what releases the lock and lets c2's insert conflict — and it yields to the event loop, so `second` **rejected with no handler attached** and Node emitted `unhandledRejection`; the later `await second` then caught it, so every assertion still passed. **A green suite and a red process, from a promise that was correct in its logic and wrong in its timing.** Fixed by settling into a tagged value in the same expression that creates the promise (`.then(ok, err)`), so no interleaving leaves it unhandled. Verified: the file alone now runs **8 passed, exit 0, zero error lines**. The production mint was never implicated — `mintFreeAllowanceIfDue` has carried `.onConflictDoNothing()` throughout, and the duplicate came from a deliberate constraint probe that inserts raw SQL twice on purpose.
- 2026-09-01 — **Why this one is worth the ledger line: it is the exact inverse of the failure mode this project keeps recording.** The standing lesson is that a green check can hide a broken guard; here a **red check hid nothing and the green tests did** — 2352 passing assertions, an entry gate that had reported PASS on this same suite one round earlier, and the only signal that anything was wrong was a non-zero exit code and two lines of stderr. Reading "all tests passed" and stopping would have shipped it. A scan for the same deferred-rejection shape across every `*.docker.test.ts` found no other instance, **and that scan's limits are stated rather than implied: it only matches promises bound from a `.query(` call**, so the suite's own exit code — not the scan — is what stands as proof for the class.

## Slice 6 — reviewer gates, round 2 (2026-09-01): every round-1 finding closed, and eleven of fourteen new ones were made BY the round-1 fixes

Four reviewers again, main tree, scoped to the fix diff. Reviewer spend: **4 agents round 1 + 4 round 2 = 8.**

| Gate | Round-2 verdict | Findings | Round-1 findings |
|---|---|---|---|
| Respin billing & credits | NEEDS CHANGES | 0 BLOCK · 4 CHANGE · 3 NOTE — Almost / B | **all 5 fixed** |
| Respin brain tenancy | NEEDS CHANGES | 0 BLOCK · 3 CHANGE · 6 NOTE — Almost / B | **all 3 fixed** |
| Respin spin compliance | **BLOCK** | 1 BLOCK · 3 CHANGE · 2 NOTE — Not yet / D | **all 8 fixed** |
| Respin learning honesty | NEEDS CHANGES | 0 BLOCK · 4 CHANGE · 3 NOTE — Almost / B | 4 of 5 fixed |

**Every round-1 finding is fixed on the production path**, and three of the four reviewers proved it by EXECUTING the pipeline rather than reading it — the compliance gate ran six `tsx` probes against real source, the learning gate rebuilt the R21 harness and ran its own lying screen, the billing gate ran three suites. **Of round 2's fourteen CHANGEs, eleven are defects the round-1 fixes introduced.** That is the convergence stop-rule's own trigger, and it was named rather than absorbed.

### The round-2 BLOCK: a rule that was inert on the only field it was written for

The fifth deterministic rule — added in round 1 to make REQ-I04/I05 real controls instead of prompt lines — reads `HARD_CLAIM_FIELD_PREFIXES = ["/whyThisPerforms/"]`, and `enforcementFor` takes the **lower** of the shape ceiling and the field ceiling. Every concealment shape is `hard` at the shape level, and `/disclosure/guidance` is **not on the field list**, so all seven collapsed to `flag` on the only field they will ever land on. Executed: `status: usable, drafts: 1, hardRules: []`, with *"Most people skip the label on a short like this, and nobody needs to know a tool helped."* rendered under the product's own **Disclosure** heading. REQ-I05 is a release gate, and `claims.ts:9-11` names that exact sentence as the reason the file exists. **Two aggravating facts:** the concealment family had **no live hard path at all** (its only hard field is one where concealment vocabulary never occurs, so its hard half was witnessed by a synthetic fixture — the vacuous-witness shape); and **`claims.test.ts:195` is TITLED *"a CONCEALMENT line keeps its full strength wherever it lands"* while its body asserts `flag` for `/disclosure/guidance`** — a test named for the property, pinning its absence.

### Three findings that were the SAME defect class recurring inside its own fix

1. **Billing:** the F5 fix windowed the uncharged-attempt count at 60 minutes and left `errors.ts` and `billing-errors.ts` both asserting *"THE COUNT IS LIFETIME … the copy must not imply the cap ages out"*. The sentence a creator reads on a money screen — *"There is nothing for you to change"* — now **withholds the only remedy that exists**. Created inside the fix for R-67, the entry titled "four claims that outlived their code".
2. **Learning:** `forbidden-claims.ts`'s block header claims *every* pattern demands the interpolated half. The reviewer built its OWN lying screen — plausible static copy with plausible numbers rather than blanks — and got **7 of 8 to pass the shipped harness**; two of the eight have no interpolated half **by construction**. Round 1's F3 class, recurring inside round 1's F3 fix.
3. **Tenancy:** the provenance fix **changed `activateVoice`'s failure mode from wrong-provenance to wrong-VOICE.** With a prior coherent activation on file, `brainDocsByIds` reads the snapshot's ids unfiltered by status and returns the **superseded** voice document — the creator activates v2 and the product keeps writing in v1's voice, silently, with no signal on any surface.

### What the gates ruled on the two questions put to them

- **`viral` (compliance):** the bare-token demotion is **accepted** — it was measured refusing an honest sentence and a refusal is debited — but `reach` and `performance` each keep a **predicate** shape alongside their bare noun, and virality got the demotion **without the complementary promotion**. `"This hook goes viral…"` renders. Ruling: add a `goes viral` hard shape, keep bare `viral` at flag. R20 names three words; this restores the third.
- **The stranded `vendor_complete` orphan (billing AND tenancy, independently):** **narrowing the warrant is sufficient; the orphan is not the finding.** Billing: no credit is granted, debited or lost, and vendor spend is durably in `model_usage`, so REQ-G05 margin stays honest. Tenancy checked the three things that would have made it escalate and none held — deletion is real (`ON DELETE cascade`, verified in the migration), there is no product reader of `candidate` outside `generate.ts`, and a stranded row is uncharged to the creator.

### The honesty hole that was NOT vocabulary tuning

The learning gate executed `scanOutputClaims` over `/whyThisPerforms/reasoning` and found the **own-baseline class undetected entirely**: `"This beats your baseline."`, `"This performs better than your last three posts."`, `"It is proven to work for this audience."` and `"Your best-performing hook shape is this one."` produce **no finding at all** — not even a flag — at n = 0, which is PRD §5 metric 2 stated as fact and the exact R-10 direction. **The shapes were already written** in `studio-ui.test.tsx:1623-1625` and enforced on the product's STATIC copy, and never reached the runtime rule on model text: one vocabulary, two enforcement points, one of them missed. The 2026-08-29 population lesson.

### Two claims in `decisions.md` that this session recorded and the gates measured FALSE

Both are the orchestrator's own, and both are the same failure: a claim verified in one dimension and not in the one that mattered. **R-65's "provably transient"** was checked against the CHECK constraint and not against the lifecycle — nothing bounds how long a row stays in `vendor_complete`, and the only thing that clears one is a retry with the same `attemptId` while the action mints a fresh one per press. **R-68's closing sentence** — *"a forecast built from one of those nouns is still refused"* — was checked at the level of its line references, which were correct, and never RUN: `"This will get you more views than your last post."` returns `flag`. R-68's own preamble claims every reference was re-read in the same action that wrote it; the *citations* were, the *behaviour* was not. Golden rule 1, twice, in one slice, in the records rather than the code.

### The owner's call, taken at the two-round bound

Presented with the convergence signal, the owner ruled: **fix the BLOCK and the bounded truth corrections, do not open a third review round, and treat the claims/traceability vocabulary as a KNOWN-INCOMPLETE recall aid with a revisit trigger rather than as a complete control.** Tuning a heuristic vocabulary by review round does not converge — each round finds new strings and each addition creates new false-positive surface — and slice 6's acceptance does not rest on it. The own-baseline gap is in scope regardless, because it is a population defect (shapes that already exist, enforced in one place and not the other) rather than open-ended tuning.
- 2026-09-01 — **Round-2 fix pass, group A (`packages/modes`) complete: the BLOCK is closed and the fix went wider than the finding.** `/disclosure/` joins the hard field list and a **THIRD ceiling** — a context/negation guard — is added, so `enforcementFor` takes the lowest of shape × field × context. The guard **demotes to `flag` and never deletes**, deliberately: a misfiring guard then cannot silently allow concealment, where a deleting one could. Measured in both directions rather than argued — a widened 42-sentence probe reports **42/42 correct**. It **found a false refusal the reviewer's own 7-sentence probe missed** (*"You cannot skip the label on a paid partnership."* — `cannot` is one word and slipped both negation branches) and **closed a pre-existing live false refusal nobody had reported**: *"Nothing here is guaranteed."* was `hard` before this pass and is `flag` now, which is a sentence the product should be encouraged to write. Comma-form antithesis went **1/7 → 7/7** with all five pinned true negatives still clean.
- 2026-09-01 — **Group A's mutation matrix had a SURVIVOR and used it correctly.** 15 planted, 14 red, **M5 survived** — and rather than reporting 14/15 and moving on, it traced the survivor to its own over-claiming comment and corrected the docblock and the test to the measured truth, then wrote M15 to witness the half that is actually load-bearing. The 2026-08-26 rule applied by an author to their own matrix. Fixing the comma axis also forced a fix at the cause one level down: two shapes now both match `it`-comma lines, so `honestRefusal` was counting *findings* and printing "antithesis at 2 places" about one place — it now counts **distinct fields**, and the same miscount was found and handed on in `claimsHeading`, in a file group A could not reach.

VALUES (x, x)` on this repo's PGlite build, because Postgres checks the new row against itself after the insert; and `generations_parent_id_immutable` is a plpgsql `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger, **the repo's first**, because a CHECK cannot see a row's previous value and a writer-set scan is a scan over our own source that says nothing about a hand-run UPDATE. - 2026-09-01 — **A settlement hole closed that was correct until this slice and would have silently mispriced money afterwards.** `op` was built from `params` and carried into `SettlementCtx` — sound while every generation was mode-priced, and a hole the moment a revision was priced differently: **a retry re-submitting without the flag would have settled a stored revision candidate at the parent mode's price.** `settle` now derives the priced operation from `candidate.request.parentGenerationId` — the resolved read, not a caller flag. `hashRequest` gained `parentGenerationId` (6 fields, not 5) so a revision and an original carrying the same note cannot collide, and `CANDIDATE_VERSION` went 1 → 2 because settlement now reads two new candidate fields and a lenient v1 read would price a revision as an original.

Taking a generation id would have required a scoped generation-by-id reader `@respin/db` does not have — which would have had to be written raw inside `packages/credits`, falsifying `isolation.test.ts`'s "generate owns NO query of its own".
- 2026-09-01 — **An R17 defect found by MEASURING rather than asserting, which is what the brief asked for and is why it was found.** `eligibleFrameworks()` was unbounded: `PRIVATE_FRAMEWORK_COUNT_MAX = 50` against `FRAMEWORK_TEXT_MAX × FRAMEWORK_LIST_MAX` makes one row ~100k characters, so **a Pro creator could put over 1MB of framework text into every generation** — §7's latency budget breached by a creator's own data, on every call. `FRAMEWORK_CONTEXT_CHAR_BUDGET = 20_000` bounds it, whole rows only (half a mechanism is a corrupted mechanism), with `continue` rather than `break` so one oversized private row cannot hide the entire curated library. Measured: a hooks prompt is **2,643 chars without the library and 7,941 with all nine seeded frameworks — the library adds 5,298**, ~1.3-1.5k input tokens, leaving roughly 4x headroom under the budget.

- 2026-09-01 — **THE MONEY FLAKE IS NOT UNEXPLAINED. THE BILLING GATE REPRODUCED IT DETERMINISTICALLY AGAINST REAL POSTGRES 17, AND IT IS A LIVE REVENUE DEFECT.** `with-workspace.ts:1549-1551`'s comment claims `(created_at, attempt_id)` is "a total order, so exactly one attempt in any set has zero predecessors **whatever the interleaving**". **That is false.** `model_usage.created_at` is `clock_timestamp()` assigned at **INSERT** time, but visibility is decided at **COMMIT** time, and the two can disagree because `recordUsage` commits in its own transaction *before* the workspace lock is taken. The reproduced sequence: A inserts (earlier timestamp) but has not committed → B inserts, commits, takes the lock, sees no committed earlier row → **B takes the included build** → A commits, takes the lock, sees B's row as *later* → **A takes the included build too**. Both free, **one debit missing**. Measured output: `priors seen: att-b = 0, att-a = 0 → REPRODUCED: BOTH attempts have ZERO predecessors → charges [0,0]`.

(2) The direction is **revenue-lossy for us and never an overcharge to a creator** — the failure mode is the safe one for the customer. (3) It is **PRE-EXISTING and outside slice 7**: `inference.ts` and `inference-race.docker.test.ts` are not in the 104-file manifest, so **it does not block this slice** — but it must stop being recorded as a flake. Named fix: allocate the ordering key under the workspace lock, or replace the timestamp race with a durable per-profile "included build consumed" marker (a partial unique on `(profile_id, purpose)`) decided inside the same locked transaction as the debit — and correct the "whatever the interleaving" sentence.

## Lessons OFFERED for the owner's approval — never appended to CLAUDE.md silently

Slice 7 produced two candidates. **Neither has been written to `CLAUDE.md`**; the owner approves or declines each. If the Lessons list is full (~10), the weakest existing entry retires or merges rather than the list growing.

**Candidate 1 — offered by the final app fixer, in its own words:**

> `2026-09-02 — A RESCUE is a hole in the guard it rescues, so state its predicate and ask what ELSE satisfies it: set equality contains literal identity, so the fix that stopped honest reversed hooks being refused silently passed the same hook copied three times (why: `sameContentWords` was added with a driver for the false positive and none for the class its own predicate admitted, and the floor twelve lines above pinned the identical short pair as acceptable).`

**Candidate 2 — offered by the orchestrator, on the strength of six occurrences in one slice:**

> `2026-09-02 — When a guard's population is DERIVED, derive it from the source that defines the class and assert the derivation itself; a population widened by hand is a guard that covers instances (why: the 2026-08-29 population lesson fired SIX times inside slice 7 — a vacuous mode witness, a no-stream scan one screen wide, an honesty canon blind to its third text source, a raw-reader scan blind to drizzle's relational API, a config pin covering one key of five, and a hand-kept pause-exemption list — and TWICE the fix FOR that lesson was itself written one-instance-wide; the passes that actually closed it derived the population from source and asserted the derivation, and one of them refused its own author's rewrite.)`

**Two more observations that are NOT offered as Lessons** — they are already covered by existing entries and are recorded here as evidence rather than as new rules: **three separate vacuous tests** in one slice, each caught by a different reader (2026-07-30's "assert it in a test or delete the claim" and 2026-08-26's mutation-matrix entry cover this); and **three fixtures standing in for an order production does not produce** (a specialisation of the same).

## The included-build revenue race — owner-directed, ahead of slice 8 (2026-09-02)

- 2026-09-02 — **started, on the owner's explicit instruction ("fix the revenue race first").** This is a **pre-existing defect, outside slice 7's manifest**, reproduced deterministically against real Postgres 17 by the round-2 billing gate. It is taken ahead of a third review round and ahead of slice 8.
- 2026-09-02 — **Two facts established by the orchestrator in the source before any brief was written, because both change the severity.** (1) **The race is REACHABLE on every tier**: `run-slot.ts` is a **per-WORKSPACE** slot sized 2 Free / 2 Creator / 4 Pro / 8 Studio, so it does not serialise two concurrent first-ever builds on one profile. (2) **There is a THIRD consumer of the ordering semantics** — `spend-rollup.ts:291-331` re-implements the ranking and says in terms that *"THE RANKED POPULATION MUST MATCH `countBillableAttempts`'s EXACTLY"* — so the two `inference.ts` call sites (`:553` pre-call price, `:838` debit) and the rollup are **one population**, and a fix that changes two of three silently breaks the reconciliation report. Both facts are in the implementer's brief as things to confirm rather than trust.
- 2026-09-02 — **A safety net was taken before any edit, because ALL of slice 7 is uncommitted and this fix edits `with-workspace.ts`, which slice 7 also changed.** The restore point on disk is still `b2c7539`, which predates slice 7 entirely. Snapshot in the session scratchpad: a **13,531-line `git diff HEAD` patch** plus **all 44 untracked slice-7 files copied path-preserving** (the first `tar` attempt failed silently and was caught by counting the result rather than trusting the exit — 44 of 44 verified). **The owner has been told plainly that committing slice 7 would be the better protection, and that offer stands.**
- 2026-09-02 — **The mechanism, recorded so the fix is judged against it rather than against a summary.** `model_usage.created_at` is `clock_timestamp()` at **INSERT**; visibility is decided at **COMMIT**; and R11 deliberately commits the usage row in its own transaction **before** the debit's lock is taken. So: A inserts (earlier timestamp) without committing → B inserts, commits, takes the lock, sees no committed earlier row → **B free** → A commits, takes the lock, sees B's row as *later* by the key → **A free**. `priors seen: att-b = 0, att-a = 0`. **The VALUES are a total order; the READER'S SNAPSHOT is not** — which is precisely the distinction the two comments asserting the opposite (`with-workspace.ts:1549-1551`, `inference.ts:819-841`) failed to make, while citing a docker test as proof.
- 2026-09-02 — **Direction recorded honestly: revenue-lossy for us, NEVER an overcharge to a creator.** That is why it was not an emergency and is not a reason to leave it.
- 2026-09-02 — **The brief names a preferred fix and a REJECTED one with its reason, so the implementer can overturn either with evidence.** Preferred: make the entitlement a **durable claim whose uniqueness the database enforces**, claimed inside the same locked transaction as the debit — decided at commit time, so no interleaving yields two winners, and matching this repo's existing `credit_ledger_free_allowance_uq` and `brain_docs` partial-index shapes. Rejected: moving the `model_usage` insert inside the locked transaction, which would close the race but **revert R11's deliberate ordering** (spend recorded even when the debit refuses or the process dies) — allowed only on a showing that R11's property survives.
- 2026-09-02 — **Four properties the fix must not break, all of them previously-closed defects:** R11's commit-before-debit; `consumedIncludedBuild`, which exists because **a truncated reply caused by our own reply ceiling was counting as the creator's free build** (2026-08-29); agreement between the pre-call price, the debit price and the rollup's ranking; and the debit staying inside the workspace-locked transaction over an append-only ledger. Migration discipline pinned to **B-4's lesson** — `0012` set `NOT NULL` plus a non-empty CHECK with no backfill and no `NOT VALID`/`VALIDATE` split, which aborts a deploy mid-migration — and **the backfill is called out as load-bearing in the opposite direction: without it, every creator who has already used their free build silently gets a second.**
- 2026-09-02 — **The acceptance bar is a RUNNING RACE, not a passing suite**: a new case in `inference-race.docker.test.ts` driving the exact interleaving, **failing deterministically on today's code** and passing after — with the fix's reversion planted and confirmed red. The brief says why in one line: *this defect exists because a claim was reasoned and never run.*
